ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u5	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. I'm opening a discussion. Please take the floor of Mr Grzegorz Matusiak, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u6	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Please adopt at second reading a draft government bill amending the Law on Biocomponents and Liquid biofuels and some other laws, prints No 2411 and 2411-A. The Law, which will create the Low Emissions Transport Fund, provides a real tool to combat air pollution from the transport sector. The explanations and analyses of the Ministry of Energy and the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management presented at the committee meetings proved the need for a dedicated instrument, the Low Emission Transport Fund. The arguments presented also show that the fund can be created thanks to the current situation in the fuel market. The government's fight against the grey liquid fuel zone has helped fuel sector operators achieve very good financial results and has also supported a high level of economic growth in Poland. These actions promote the creation of a fund aimed at financing the transformation of the Polish transport sector towards low- and zero-emission transport. The Fund will be a source of funding for Polish companies and local governments wishing to be involved in ensuring clean transport. It may also finance clean transport modes for which electricity, hydrogen or natural gas are used as propulsion. I would like to stress that the Low Emission Transport Fund will be a key element in achieving the objective set out in the Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels, i.e. to ensure that Polish society is clean, healthier and more environmentally friendly for transport. Moreover, for the first time in Poland the neglected problems of pollution generated by the transport sector have been solved in such a comprehensive way over the years. We have both a law regulating the alternative fuels market - the law on electromobility and alternative fuels - and this law, which provides funding for the entire programme. Long discussions during committee meetings confirmed that the system is a coherent whole. It provides not only the rules needed for operators in the sector, but also the financing of well-selected activities which these operators can obtain. This therefore creates a kind of industrial programme that fits into the general economic strategy of the government. The fund dedicated to the transport area is a testimony of Poland's commitment to the European and global trend of supporting low- and zero-emission mobility. Intensified action in this field is taken not only by the most developed countries, such as the United States, Japan, China and India, but also in Europe - the Netherlands, Denmark and Germany, but also by countries closer to Poland in terms of economic development: Slovenia and the Czech Republic. Therefore, Poland should not be left behind, but in this race take the place due to the size of its economy and its importance in Europe as well as in the world. The Low Carbon Transport Fund represents an important step towards this objective. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u55	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u56	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the club Law and Justice I will present the position of our club regarding the draft law amending the Act on Investment in the Construction of the Waterway connecting Zalev Wiślany with the Gulf of Gdańsk, print No. 2560. At yesterday's committee meeting, of course, printing 2499 was considered. We were all in agreement about his acceptance, hence today in the report the mention of this. The amendment of the Law of 24 February 2017 on investments in the construction of a waterway linking Zalew Wiślany with the Gulf of Gdansk results from the need to adapt to the provisions of the Act of 20 July 2017. Water Law, which entered into force on 1 January 2018, in particular as regards the competence of the newly established State Water Farm Polskie. The new Water Law introduces a reform of water management in Poland and thus changes the structure of public administrations responsible for water management. The consequence of this is, among others. acquisition by the State Water Farm of Poland of the duties of the marshals of voivodships in the field of water management, including the issue of water law permits. The proposed amendment, which takes into account the above-mentioned amendments, adapts the provisions of the Act to the new Water Law, which will allow for the smooth preparation and implementation of the investment Mon. The construction of a waterway connecting Vistula Bay and Gdansk Bay. The provisions of the proposed law will apply to entities involved in the implementation of investments carried out on the basis of specialization, including the Maritime Office in Gdynia and the State Water Farm of Poland, in connection with the issue of water permits. The draft law also amends the law by introducing appropriate references to the Act of 20 July 2017. Water law together with the publicists and the relevant editorial units. The Law and Justice Club supports the project and we hope that it will be adopted in the future as it was presented in committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The project is included in Seym printing No 2497. The proposed amendment to the Law on Public Use and Volunteering makes it possible to improve the process of carrying out tasks of particular importance and sensitivity commissioned by the government administration. The most important proposed amendment concerns the determination of the maximum possible grant value of 80%. the estimated value of the investment, without, of course, taking into account the resources from the sources of the European Union. The project will have an impact on NGOs and organisations referred to in Article. 3 (1) (a) 3 Act on public benefit and voluntary activities, by enabling them to obtain more financial support than previously for public investment tasks where this is justified either for the protection of human life or health or for an important social interest or for an important public interest. Ladies and gentlemen! The project was positively assessed by the Council of Public Use Activities. The project has no impact on the labour market. The amended act is to enter into force 14 days after the date of the announcement. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u87	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and the circle. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u88	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the government draft law amending the Law on Public Benefit and Volunteering, print No 2497. The proposed amendment will facilitate the implementation of tasks of particular importance and sensitivity commissioned by the government. In accordance with current legislation, the President of the Council of Ministers, if necessary, may, for the protection of human life or health or, for reasons of important social interest or important public interest, order, inter alia. non-governmental organisations to carry out public tasks without an open competition. At present, the grant for such tasks may not exceed 50% the planned value of the investment. The government project proposed that grants of up to 80% should be granted where this public task is investment-oriented the planned cost estimate of the investment. High Seym! Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u109	The discussion is closed. We will proceed to the third reading of the bill in the voting block. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u110	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Mr. Director! I have the honour to present a report by the Health Committee on the Government's draft law amending the Pharmaceutical Law and some other laws, prints No. 2386 and 2541. The purpose of the draft law is to amend the Law of 6 September 2001 on pharmaceutical law and certain other laws, e.g. the law on medicinal activities of 15 April 2011, of course in selected thematic areas. The reason for the changes is primarily the partial implementation of EU law, i.e. 2001 EU Directive the European Parliament and the Council on medicinal products for human use. The scope of the proposed changes concerns, in general, the manufacture and marketing of medicinal products, as well as the supervision of medicinal products. Issues related to the prevention of conflicts of interest are also regulated. I'll try to discuss the most important things about the bill. That's a lot, I've chosen the issues that I consider to be the most important and interesting. Thus, the draft law refers to adding to the dictionary terms the definition of the responsible person and the competent person to order the provisions of the amended law. The proposer also proposes to introduce a definition of advanced therapy medicinal product within the WPF - a hospital exception, and also aims to define detailed rules and procedures for the production of advanced therapy medicinal product - a hospital exception, including requirements for the recruitment of a competent person and the qualifications that this person would have, which is a change compared to the previous law. In addition, the proposed law proposes to allow pharmacists with 3 years of employment - e.g. at the pharmacy, the pharmaceutical wholesaler - for the competition for the position of Inspector for Human Rights. wholesale marketing in the Chief Pharmaceutical Inspectorate. Here's a little comment. We suffer from a deep shortage of inspectors, so increasing their numbers will improve the effectiveness of the pharmaceutical inspection. Without getting pharmacists from the pharmaceutical market, it is simply not possible. The following provisions concern the so-called. conflicts of interest. That is, the proposed law introduces solutions to ensure that persons responsible for the supervision of medicinal products, including even rapporteurs or experts, have no connection, family or financial, or many others, with the pharmaceutical industry. The draft law regulates it very closely and defines it. This is all intended to ensure a transparent and independent assessment of the entrepreneur supervised by the State Pharmaceutical Inspection. The persons responsible for the supervision of medicinal products will be required to make a declaration on the absence of conflict of interest under criminal responsibility for making a false declaration. I would like to say that similar solutions or requirements are applied by the European Medicines Agency. The next group of provisions that we propose to amend are, in general terms, anti-export records. Namely, the project bans the combination of economic activities related to the marketing of medicinal products with the pursuit of medicinal activities. It's been possible so far. No provision prohibiting mergers e.g. wholesaler of medicinal products or wholesaler of medicinal products with medicinal activities is an important legal gap. This resulted, among other things. Uprising, as experts call it, so-called. The reverse chain of distribution, namely traders or medicinal entities purchased medicines and then via a pharmaceutical wholesaler, having agreed to sell drugs, sold drugs abroad, often at prices several times higher than the purchase price. The procedure was mostly about medicines refunded, most often life-saving medicines and expensive medicines. Effect: worsening the availability of medicines, and often lacking for Polish patients in our Polish pharmacies. Antagonists of this solution will say that the proposed changes restrict freedom of management, which is of course a constitutional value, but they are also justified among constitutional premises, e.g. Article 2 Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 Constitution - Protection of public health or Article. 22 Constitutions - an important social interest. The consequence of this amendment is the introduction in the amended law of provisions on declarations under criminal liability. The Act also regulates the area of clinical trials in the field of medicinal products. The most important effect of the proposed regulation will be to replace the requirement to include contracts concluded by sponsors with persons involved in the clinical trial as part of the pre-documented requirement to provide a brief description of the financing of the clinical trial. In this argument, I will simply say that the Republic of Poland is currently the only country of the European Union where such requirements exist. This results in an extension of the period of clinical trials and the outflow of these clinical trials from Poland, which is of course not beneficial. The period is statistically prolonged by an average of several months. Currently, we are not an attractive market for sponsors to conduct clinical trials and this law is to change that. Then I wanted to talk about an area that regulates pharmaceutical law. Namely, it defines the obligation for pharmacy and pharmaceutical wholesalers or hospital pharmacy departments to provide data on the marketing of medicinal products to - that is such a difficult name - the Integrated Monitoring System for the Marketing of Medicinal Products. This requirement was introduced by the Law of 9 April 2015 with effect from 1 January 2017, but the law in this case was dead and the entities were not prepared to carry out their duties formally rested on them. As a result, the Act proposes to withdraw the reporting obligation: we are faced with actual abolition until 30 September 2018. To conclude, I will say that the bill enters into force 14 days after its announcement, with a few exceptions which I will not discuss here, because everything is included in the project. The proposed law does not entail additional costs from the State budget. The project is not contrary to EU law. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On 29 March 2018, the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Health Commission. The Health Committee after first reading and considering this project on 11 and 12 April this year. and 10 May 2018 asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u112	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the club Law and Justice on the government draft law amending the pharmaceutical law and some other laws with draft implementing acts, print no.2386 and 2541. The current rules need to be fully aligned with EU rules. There is a need to ensure their full compliance with the provisions of Directive 2001/83/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 6 November 2001 on the Community code relating to medicinal products for human use. The project clarifies in this respect, inter alia. regulation on the manufacture of advanced treatment medicinal products for hospital exceptions, including, inter alia,: introduces a definition of an advanced hospital exception medicinal product; provides the basis for the GIF to establish the expiry of the authorisation to manufacture or import a medicinal product; provides for the approval by the competent authority of a Member State of the conditions for the manufacture of an advanced hospital exception medicinal product; sets out what should include an application for authorisation to manufacture such products; also sets out requirements for a competent person who will be responsible for ensuring that each series of advanced hospital exception medicinal product has been manufactured and checked in accordance with the provisions of the amended law. Another problem solved is that Poland is currently the only Member State of the European Union which requires the submission, together with a request for a clinical trial, of clinical trial agreements concluded between the parties involved in the clinical trial. In view of the above, the obligation to attach to the application for the initiation of a clinical trial which the sponsor submits to the President of the Office for the Registration of Medicinal Products, Medical Devices and Biocidal Products and to the Bioethical Committee, the clinical trial agreements concluded between the parties involved in the clinical trial has been amended. In accordance with the proposed regulations, the application will be accompanied, in particular, by information on the level of remuneration of the investigators and subjects of the clinical trial or compensation for them and the agreement on the clinical trial between the sponsor and the centre. The proposal also includes the addition of new legislation on the State Pharmaceutical Inspection to prevent conflicts of interest. This is also a requirement of the directive. In order to maintain transparency and prevent abuse, the provisions prohibit persons conducting proceedings within the framework of the State Pharmaceutical Inspection to be associated with supervised entities. Here we have also supported an appropriate amendment extending the catalogue of such people. The aim of the project is also to suspend the application by 30 September 2018 of the rules on the imposition of fines on traders marketing medicinal products, refunded foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and refunded medical products and the obligations of transmitting certain data on the marketing of medicinal products, the refunded foodstuffs for special nutritional uses and medical devices to the Integrated Monitoring System for the Marketing of Medicinal Products on the territory of the Republic of Poland. This change results from the fact that not all entrepreneurs were prepared to report data using this system at the time from which the system was originally intended to function, i.e. 1 January 2017 The draft law will improve the functioning of the system of supervision and marketing of medicinal products. The introduction of new rules on conflicts of interest will improve the transparency of inspections, prevent corruption and implement Directive 2001/83/EC. Since these are good and necessary for the implementation of the solution, the Law and Justice Club declares its support for the proposed project. At the same time, I would like to make four appropriate amendments to your marshal on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u114	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the Civic Platform to the government's draft law amending the pharmaceutical law and certain other laws. The next amendment concerns the suspension of the obligation to transmit data through the Integrated Monitoring System for the Marketing of Medicinal Products and the need to postpone the reporting deadline as a result of the failure to implement this monitoring system before 30 September 2018. And the point concerning the ban on a single trader's conduct of several economic activities also aroused a lot of controversy and comments. This applies to the possibility of combining the activity of medicinal products with the marketing of medicinal products. It has been indicated that medicinal products refunded by companies registered in the Register of Medicinal Entities are exported abroad and are authorised to operate pharmaceutical wholesalers at the same time. There is no indication of how many such cases have been reported and what actions have been taken in view of the fact that such practices are illegal activities. With such restrictions, the facts and volume of illegal exports of medicinal products should be described in detail, since it is difficult to refer to the content of the justification for these changes in the Act, since it does not contain quantitative data on the identified phenomenon of illicit exports of medicinal products. There is also a lack of information as to whether and to what extent the measures taken so far, including the revision of the law aimed at reducing and eliminating the above-mentioned practice, are effective. In the draft under consideration we have further provisions, this time decisively limiting certain economic activities which are intended to combat the effect and not the cause of the cost-effectiveness of such exports from the country of medicinal products. The impact assessment attached to the justification of the draft law also lacks an estimate of its financial and economic impact on those covered by the merger ban. There are also no figures provided as to how many such entities are currently linked, which is eliminated in the project, medicinal activities and activities in the marketing of medicinal products. Only on the basis of detailed data in this respect will it be possible to assess whether the proposed provisions restricting freedom of business are adequate to the situation on the market for medicinal products in Poland and whether they are proportionate to the risks. In view of the above doubts and the lack of opportunity to listen to a social party that was not invited to the proceedings of the Health Committee, and we have been waiting for the position of the social side, our committee could not read its arguments, the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will refrain from voting. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u115	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mr Jerzy Kozłowski, Kukiz Club15.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u116	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Kukiz Club, I have the honour to present our position on the draft law amending the pharmaceutical law and certain other laws, seym prints No. 2386 and 2541. The Act partially implements Directive 2001/83/EC on the Community code relating to medicinal products for human use. The Chief Pharmaceutical Inspector will be able to conclude the expiry of the authorisation for the manufacture or import of medicinal products. Specific requirements for advanced therapy medicinal products have been laid down. A completely new regulation is envisaged for the change of consent to the manufacture of advanced therapy medicinal products. Manufacturers and importers of medicinal products will have to apply the requirements of good distribution practice as regards the distribution of medicinal products. An ad hoc inspection is foreseen not only in the manufacturer or importer of the medicinal product but also in the responsible entity or its representative in the event of a reasonable suspicion of the quality or safety risk of the medicinal products used. In the context of the procedure for examining applications for authorisation to operate wholesale pharmaceutical medicinal products for human use, the GIF will evaluate their transhipment chambers in the context of an assessment of the suitability of the premises and not, so far, the voivodships of pharmaceutical inspectors. In addition to providing the PIF authorities with information on suspicion or finding that the medicinal product does not meet the quality requirements set for it, the pharmacy manager will also be tasked with providing information on the suspicion or finding that the medicinal product does not meet the quality requirements set for it. providing information on the suspicion that the product has been falsified. For the competitions for the positions of inspectors for: wholesale GIFs will be able to join pharmacists with 3 years of experience working at the pharmacy. Currently, only the person who works in wholesale traders can be an inspector. Persons carrying out business activities in the framework of the activities of the PIF will have to make declarations on their relationship with the pharmaceutical industry. The Heads of the PIF and its inspectors and other persons acting on behalf of the PIF, including expert experts, will have to make declarations on the absence of conflicts of interest. The Act also introduces amendments to the merger of certain activities. It prohibits a single trader from carrying out several economic activities in which it may purchase medicinal products. A wholesale trader will not be able to carry out a therapeutic activity at the same time. The law then amends the rules on the conduct of clinical trials. There is the abolition of bureaucratic obligations, which result in the need to submit together with a proposal to launch a clinical trial of certain documents and to carry out activities by bioethical committees which are not necessary to carry out it, but are very often laborious. The obligation to transfer data and information to an integrated system for monitoring the marketing of medicinal products is postponed during the period until 30 September 2018 as well as the obligation to transfer data from Responsible Entities and Entrepreneurs to that system. What are the expectations of the bill? First of all, it is about better protection of public health by strengthening the system of supervision and marketing of medicinal products, as well as reducing the export of drugs abroad in order to increase their availability to patients in Poland and to improve access to new drug therapies. I'd like to stop for a moment on the subject of illegal drug export. It is important to bear in mind that this practice has a long history. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u132	Thank you very much. High Izbo! On behalf of the Joint Commission: Protection of the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and Energy and Treasury, I present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Investments in Condensed Natural Gas Regasification Terminal in Świnoujście and some other laws, prints No. 2504 and 2562. The project was addressed to first reading on 8 May this year, and on 16 May the first reading of the joint committee was held: environmental protection and energy. High Izbo! The purpose of the Government of the Republic of Poland is to ensure that gas supply from alternative sources to Russian sources is possible, so that it is possible not to extend the Jamaican contract, which is due to expire in 2022. Dependence on the dominant supplier is linked to the risk of restrictions or interruptions in gas supply. We have to end this condition. In order for Poland to become independent, it is necessary to make investments that will enable gas to be imported from new directions and to distribute this gas across the country through the Polish transmission system. High Izbo! The necessary investments include, first, the expansion of the regasification terminal of liquefied natural gas. President Lech Kaczyński in Świnoujście, so that it can be increased capacity, the provision of bunkering services for ships and the transhipment of liquefied natural gas into railway containers, and secondly, the connection of Poland with gas deposits in the Norwegian continental shelf by the Danish transmission system, which will enable the supply of about 10 billion m3 of gas per year, i.e. approx. 60% Polish gas demand. The existing legal solutions contained in the 2009 terminal specialty are not sufficient and will not allow all necessary investments to be made within the desired period in such a way as to ensure continuous, stable and secure supplies and the transmission of natural gas. Changes are needed, in particular those contained in the Article. 16, Art. 19a-19d, Art. 20, 22 and 24, articles. 25a, Art. 27, Art. 36a and Article 36a 38 of this bill. The proposed changes lead to a significant reduction in the time taken to prepare and implement the investment. First, the decision to determine the location of the terminal investment is the final act. It addresses all issues in such a way that administrative authorities do not consider them. once again resolved cases for proceedings to be carried out by the competent authorities, as well as to limit timeless handling of cases. The new rules will facilitate the acquisition of property rights, the introduction of temporary restrictions on the use of real estate or the acquisition of service by the investor. In particular, amendments to the Article. 16 provide that the rules on the removal of trees and shrubs contained in the Environmental Law shall not apply to immovable property covered by the decision to determine the location of investments in the terminal, except in the case of immovable property entered in the Register of Monuments. In turn, the amendments to Article. 19 provide that the decision on environmental conditions and the review must be taken within 45 days of the date of receipt of the request or appeal - here are in principle changes in the scope of the appeal - the competent authority will fine PLN 1000 for each day of delay both in one case and in the other case. This will certainly improve the conduct of the proceedings. Group of articles added, i.e. Article 2 19a-19d. If for testing, measurements, archaeological, geological, hydrogeological or geotechnical tests, etc. it is necessary to enter the property of others, the investor may apply to the competent local voivode for a decision to allow the entry into the property of others. Previously, it must request its owner to agree on the manner, scope and date of use of the property. On the other hand, if the owner does not agree within 30 days, the voivode shall issue an authorisation decision, of course in favour of compensation. This use of someone else's property must not result in any interruption of road or rail traffic or the safety risks of that traffic. Art. 20 provides that the decision to determine the location shall result in the ownership of the State Treasury of the real estate located in the land-marking lines of the investment, the acquisition of perpetual use rights and property rights of buildings and permanently connected with the land of the equipment, as well as the exclusion of limited rights in rem. In turn, the provisions of the Article. 22 provide that the decision to determine the location is the basis for the decision to terminate the permanent management of the land on the Management Board of State Forest Forests and to terminate the lease, lease or loan contracts within one month. Art. 24 and 25a concern the limitation of the use of real estate. The changes introduced give the possibility to authorise the setting up and operation of ground and underground equipment, etc., while the limitation of the use of the property may also be granted to an entity other than the investor. For the land of the State Treasury, these restrictions occur without compensation, I stress: for the land of the State Treasury. Art. 27 imposes an obligation on the State Forestry Forests to cut down trees and shrubs and clean them up on properties managed by the State Forests and covered by the permission to build investments in terminal areas. Art. 36a. In accordance with these provisions, only the investor shall agree to change the final decision to determine the location without the need to obtain the consent of the other parties to the proceedings. It is obvious that in such extensive administrative proceedings the number of pages is enormous. We refer here to the article. 155 of the Code of Administrative Procedure and by analogy with Article. 36a Construction law. If this solution had not been introduced, any changes would have been extremely difficult. High Izbo! The enforcement authority implementing non-monetary obligations shall be the voivode, in accordance with the provisions of Article. 37a. High Izbo! A revised article. 38 includes the calculation of all necessary components of planned gas pipelines, nodes or gas pressing stations, connections, regasification stations, bunkering and handling bases. This list is long, expressis verbis is specified in this Article. 38. I'm not going to quote it, any of you can get to know it. High Izbo! The proposed solutions limit the rights of right-holders to immovable property located on the dividing lines only to the extent necessary to achieve this objective of construction, the intended construction of planned equipment. The interference in property rights or limited substantive rights or obligations was introduced here only in situations where no other solutions could be found. This draft contained in the report of the joint committees also provides for the protection of the interests of those whose rights are restricted. The extent of the possible activities of the investor, the area of activity of the administrative authorities and the issues of appropriate compensation for damage or loss shall be specified in detail. Thus, High Izbo, the proposed rules ensure respect for the interests of entities whose rights will be restricted in connection with the preparation or implementation of this very important investment for Poland. High Izbo! The legislative arrangements discussed are intended to ensure that the necessary equipment is completed in the spring of 2022 and that the necessary equipment is launched in the autumn of 2022. The Joint Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Energy and the Treasury adopted the report by 28 votes in favour, with no votes against, with 4 abstentions. High Izbo! On behalf of the Joint Environmental Committees and the Energy and Treasury Committee, please adopt the draft law in print No 2562. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

President Lech Kaczyński in Świnoujście, consisting of increasing terminal capacity, providing ship bunkering services, transshipment of liquefied natural gas into railway containers, as well as the connection of the Republic of Poland with gas deposits on the Norwegian Continental shelf through the Danish transmission system, which will allow delivery to Poland of about 10 billion m3 of gas per year. The proposed changes have taken advantage of past experience in the implementation of investments based on specializations. The project guarantees adequate compensation. Such solutions should be assessed positively. The proposed amendment concerns the exemption from permits for the removal of trees - and the charges for them - from the areas covered by the decision to determine the location of the investment. In this area, provision is also made for a 45-day period to consider appeals against environmental considerations decisions and penalties for non-compliance within the time limit for those decisions. The proposed changes are necessary and substantively justified in the project. Energy security is a fundamental guarantee of economic development, but also of the comfort and peaceful life of citizens. The proposed act will undoubtedly contribute to the rapid diversification of gas supply, thus ensuring the energy security of Poland. In order to achieve this objective, planned investments are necessary, which must be completed by the end of 2022, i.e. before the expiry of the Jamaican contract. The draft law submitted by the government in print 2504 is well and thoroughly justified. He received positive opinions from the combined energy committees and environmental committees and meets the challenge of the Polish state of affairs. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u164	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Seym! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy a report on the parliamentary drafts of the laws amending the Property Act 2224 and 2510. The Marshal of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent these draft laws to the Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy respectively for first reading. The Committee on Infrastructure and the Committee on Territorial Government and Regional Policy after first reading and considering these draft laws at its meeting on 5 June 2018 request the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Seym! The draft law amending the Property Act aims to eliminate interpretation doubts arising from the changes introduced by the Act of 20 July 2017 on National Property Resources to the Act of 24 June 1994 on the ownership of premises concerning the separation of independent dwellings and the competence of the old-timer to examine the grounds for the separation of independent dwellings. As from 11 September Ub. R. Article 4 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council 2 (2) 1a The law on the ownership of premises establishes a separate ownership of the self-catering premises, either according to the findings of the local spatial planning plan or the content of the decision on the conditions of construction and land-use and in accordance with the building permit or the effective notification, and according to the permit for use. A separate property in a residential and single-family building may represent at most two independent dwellings. The doubts raised in the context of the provision concern the absence of a clear determination of the addressee of the standard required to examine the compatibility of the separation of an independent dwelling according to the findings of the local plan or decision on the conditions of construction and spatial planning and other building decisions. In addition, interpretation doubts arose in the area of separation of self-contained dwellings in buildings constituting an old residential substance in cases where there is no planning and construction documentation. The proposed amendment to the House Property Act takes into account the most frequently reported problems hindering the application of the House Property Act, problems raised by municipalities, housing cooperatives, district old townships, MEPs, senators, institutions and organisations in the field of the Art. 2 (2) 1a, which concerns the release of self-contained dwellings in buildings created before the date of entry into force of this provision, i.e. before 11 September Ub. R., where no planning and construction documentation is available. Thanks to the solutions introduced, the purpose of the amendment of the Property Act will be met. It was intended to clearly define the rules for the separation of independent dwellings by taking into account the findings of the local plan, decisions on construction conditions and building decisions. The issue of self-reliance certificates before 11 September 2017 by the old lady also complicated the inconsistent issue of the case law of the administrative courts. The proposed regulation will help to improve the procedure for issuing self-certifications by limiting the obligation to examine the compatibility of the planned separation of dwellings with spatial planning and construction documents only to buildings created after 1 January 1995. At the same time, it is necessary to emphasise that irregularities in the production of premises which have become a reason for adding in Article. 2 Act on the ownership of premises. 1a, refer to buildings created in recent years. High Izbo! The draft law will not have any financial implications for the state budget or the budgets of local government units. The project has a positive social impact and is not the subject of European Union law. High Izbo! On behalf of the committee, I request the adoption of a bill. Thank you very much for your attention. A separate property in a single-family building may constitute at most two independent dwellings. Let me remind you that this provision introduced last year was intended to remove the legal gap caused by the case-law of the courts, which was extremely conflicting. Within the building of a single family building, developers simply built multi-dwelling houses and used this gap caused by the case law, secreted and sold a much larger number of housing units, resulting in conflicts with residents, inability to establish good communication services and all other types. Thus, High Izbo, the problem with the interpretation of this provision, which was amended last year in the House Property Act, is that the expressis verbis, which the entity is to test the compatibility of the way the premises are secreted with the ones mentioned in paragraph, was not defined at that time. 1a construction documents. The starosts refuse to issue certificates of compliance with the conditions laid down in Article. 2 (2) 1a Act on the ownership of premises, although it is the starosts who have the proper resources of documents, because they conduct proceedings in the field of architectural and construction administration. High Izbo! Doubtful interpretation of the rules has also arisen in the issue of self-contained dwellings in older buildings, for which no planning and construction documentation is available. I would like to remind you that the obligation to keep this documentation was created only on 1 January 1995, hence the release of independent premises in old buildings and the establishment of separate properties for them became extremely difficult. These are the problems facing two parliamentary projects from prints 2224 and 2510. It's a sign of the mouth. Article 1a The 2th House Property Act has been corrected. In addition, paragraphs 1b and 1c are added. Paragraph 1b provides that the provisions shall not apply to buildings existing before 1 January 1995 or constructed on the basis of a permit issued before that date, unless construction works requiring building permits have been carried out. It should be noted that exploiting the regulatory gap allowing for circumvention of planning and construction rules, resulting in multiple-family homes in single-family housing areas, creates numerous social conflicts and has increased in recent years. This conflictogenic situation will be countered by the provision of the mouth. 1c, which provides that a separate property in a single-family building may constitute, at most, two separate dwellings. As I say, this does not apply to buildings constructed on the basis of a permit issued before 11 July 2003. Article 3 2 provides that the Starosta, in the form of a certificate, shall certify that the requirements referred to in paragraph have been met. 1a-2. All proceedings not completed before the date of entry into force of the Act in question will be carried out in accordance with the amended provisions. High Izbo! An entry in the everlasting book is necessary to create this property. This process shall be attended by a notary or a civil court ruling on the abolition of joint ownership and a perpetual accounting court. Consequently, the entry into force of the Act on KZN, i.e. Since 11 September 2017, it is not possible to establish the grounds necessary for the effective establishment of a separate ownership of premises as notaries and courts refuse such an entry. A large part of the housing resources of the municipalities are buildings built before World War II, and a larger part of the buildings of housing cooperatives are buildings from the 1960s or 80s of the last century. This problem concerns practically all buildings that were built before 1994, as it is only since 1995 that the owner has been obliged to keep complete documentation of the post-executive construction for the period of existence of the building. Since it is not legally possible to establish a separate ownership of premises from 11 September 2017 and the legal activities involved from that date may be subject to a sanction of nullity and the threat of refusal of entries in perpetual books, it is necessary to enter into force as soon as possible of the proposed amendment to the law, because residents, courts and notaries have difficulty applying the law you are implementing. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u194	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! The main objective of this draft law is to harmonise VAT rates on widely understood foodstuffs and fruit. In the current legal situation for these articles, three rates of tax are applied on these products, i.e. 5% 7% and 22% , but in accordance with Article. The last two rates are temporarily raised to 8% and 23%, respectively, by 31 December 2018. The Civic Platform Club's project aims to harmonise VAT rates to 5% for all food currently covered by the rate of 7% - by the end of this year 8% - and food at a tax rate 22% - again by the end of this year 23% - such as coffee, tea, confectionery or chocolate. Among the main advantages of the harmonised VAT rate for food products are three. First of all, it is a simplification of the tax system, a simplification primarily for entrepreneurs. Moreover, this will certainly reduce VAT fraud, as this is a reduction of the systemic gap. And thirdly, the project will certainly protect the poorest, because this is the social group - the least earners - which spends the most on its food earnings. The differentiated VAT rates for food also constitute an administrative barrier for food business operators. On the agenda are situations where operators producing or marketing different foods are required to charge VAT of different amounts for each type of product sold. This makes it difficult to conduct business, but it also creates a room for error, which can result in losses either on the side of the State Treasury or on the side of the entrepreneur. The example of a miserable hot dog is already famous here, in which it actually focuses as in the lens the problem of differing VAT rates on food. Hot dog - i.e. roll, sausage and mustard or ketchup, which is simple, it would seem, the product - is actually all possible VAT rates. The roll itself can be taxed at three different rates, because if its shelf-life is longer than 14 days, then the rate is 23% if it is up to 14 days of shelf-life, but the sugar content is increased, then we have 8%. But if it's just a bun with a 14-day shelf-life date, then we have 5% VAT. You can definitely get lost in this. The sausage is 5%. VAT, but already mustard - 23%, mustard sauce, in fact not to know why - 8% and ketchup - also 8%. Spices: salt and pepper - 8% But it's already marjoram and dill - 23%. From the media reports a few months ago, we know that even such a great businessman as Orlen State Treasury has been selling hot dogs with a lower VAT rate for a year. It means that not only small and medium-sized enterprises have trouble here, but also giants can't handle it. Thus, the differentiation of the VAT rate for food is certainly the area for fraud related to the fraud of the refund of this tax as an indirect tax. And in our view, in the opinion of the designers, the harmonisation of this rate will reduce the risk of fraud and thus the fraud of VAT refunds. And it is worth noting that in this connection the Civic Platform club project fits into a very clear tendency to seal the fiscal system in Poland initiated by the Government of the Civic Platform and PSL and continued by the Government of Law and Justice. Our project assumes, as I have already said, a level playing field for food products such as fruit, spices, starch, coffee, tea, chocolate, confectionery, sauces, soups or bread. We have constructed the provisions of the Act exactly as they are constructed today, in Annex 10, where there are goods taxed at a rate of 5%, we add those goods that we want from the catalogue of those with a rate of 23%. and 8% For now, move to the ones with the lower stakes. We do not remove them from there because this methodology was used earlier. Of course, the law bears a certain cost for the state budget. This cost of introducing a 5% rate for non-alcoholic foods is between 1200 million and 1600 million. But I would like to stress that, because this applies to foodstuffs, the reduction in the rate is of social importance, because it should translate into the cost of purchasing these goods. And, as I have already mentioned, this will be particularly important for those groups of consumers that are the poorest. It's got it, of course. also important in the context of the price increase of many foodstuffs. It is social importance not only for the consumer, but also for small and medium-sized enterprises, for which the administrative costs associated with the high differential rates are particularly burdensome. This is linked to the complexity of the accounting system, requires additional supervision, controls, leads to classification problems, which thus exposes the taxpayer to error in tax settlement and to unpleasant consequences of this. Small and medium-sized entrepreneurs are not always able to use the services of professional law firms, especially since these services are not cheap. The differentiation of rates also requires the taxable person to keep track of the descriptions on the packaging of the product or its specifications and promotes abuse of the inclusion on the packaging of the expiry dates appropriate to the application of the lower rate. I said that the bill was a cost, but in our view, the source of funding for its implementation will be the amount of tax that will be paid in connection with the sealing of the VAT system in this area and the reduction of the grey zone. A good example is the example of Orlen, which for a year sold hot dogs with a lower VAT rate, after all, reduced the revenue of the state budget. I would also like to point out that the draft law is in line with European Union law. We got the right opinions from the Foreign Minister. ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u234	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We have proposals for four laws. I will refer to them in the order in which they were presented. The first law deals with the far-reaching simplification of the so-called. matrices of VAT rates in the field of food. It is a project that actually leads to a reduction in bureaucracy, to facilitation for entrepreneurs, as well as for administrations and courts, and in principle fits into the answer to those problems that are obvious and present in our financial law system. Of course, I am talking about the complexity of the tax system in Poland. All this is true, but I would like to remind you shyly that the current state of legislation, for example with regard to the VAT rates on food, is the result of legislation carried out in 2010 and 2011 by the then Ministry of Finance led by Minister Rostowski. From the beginning it was indicated that this change, the transition from one older PKWiU to a newer one, could be used for this simplification. Unfortunately, that didn't happen. In those years, this system was deeply complicated. In some areas it was necessary to withdraw quickly from these regulations, after a few months it had to be updated - here mentioned by Members of the Civic Platform cookies, for which a 14-day shelf life date was launched, and then, after a turbulent discussion, even protests, it had to be switched to 45-day ones. We have this condition programmed consciously, because all the arguments that have been made in this Chamber today concerning the complications of this system were present in that discussion. I remember that exactly, I had the opportunity to participate in those works as an expert for our foundation. On the other hand, the basic argument regarding the simplification of the system, which we, of course, recognise - of our work in the Ministry in a moment - must also be assessed in terms of the financial outcome of these requests. Here our calculations actually differ from the proposals contained in the justification for this amendment. We see a loss of budget revenue of PLN 3.7 billion per year. At this moment, in this budgetary situation, in this macroeconomic situation, under these external conditions: the expenditure rule, the Treaty obligations and EU law do not have the full scope to implement this request. That's the first thing. Second thing. As a financial resort, we are also working on simplification in this area, but our proposals, although they are moving towards, as I say, a significant simplification of this matrix, do not cause such a significant damage to budgetary revenue, which is an expression of responsibility for the sustainability of public finances, for the budget. This work is ongoing and I hope that it will soon be submitted for consultation. Of course, there are other very important aspects. During the debate, there were allegations of the amount of tax interpretation resulting from this complication in the VAT rate system. We agree with all of this, of course, and we are building a new way of guaranteeing taxpayers tax security related to the allocation of VAT rates to their goods, to their products, and it seems to us that these changes that we are planning will respond to these major problems of business without harming consumers. As I said, the present state of complexity, today's absurdities related to mustard, Dijon sauce or the amount of VAT rates present in the hot dog are the direct consequence of the lack of bold movements at that time, i.e. in 2010, when, in fact, due to the need to change the PKWiU, all the changes that we are working so hard on today in the resort could be made. So that the proposal, as it is written, is from the perspective of the finance department at this point impossible because of the responsibility for the state of public finances, although the direction of simplification is not so much the right direction as necessary, speaking of the whole reform of our tax system. Turning to the issue of children's clothing, I would like to begin by answering the question which was addressed to me personally. Yes, I had the pleasure to co-create the proposal for a derogation or a revision of the directive, and for that time it was the only form that gave us the legal right to apply this reduced rate to such products - let's call it - prodemographic clothing type and children's shoes. That proposal was rejected as an embarrassing proposal, as a frivolous proposal and, unfortunately, it was not realized at the level of Poland's application for such preferences in the VAT Directive. A lot has changed since then. We have been actively involved in the European Commission's work on a comprehensive change of VAT rates in the VAT Directive for at least two years. One of the basic areas on which we place significant emphasis is precisely to enable Poland and other Member States to apply reduced rates in this prodemographic area. I personally go to bilateral meetings with my counterparts, during which we exchange experiences, I present, apart from many other aspects of our reforms, how important it is to enable Poland to apply reduced rates in this area. So that this awareness is built with our partners in the European Union, and in this direction there is also work at the level of the European Commission and in those bodies that are involved in this process. Therefore, if we were to apply unilaterally, contrary to the provisions of the Directive Today, this rate reduced to this prodemographic commodity group could jeopardise the positive finality of our efforts for two years in this area at European Union level. Therefore, there is a request for some kind of patience. I hope that soon we will be able to apply the reduced rate fully legally, fully in accordance with EU regulations. All these arguments, which you have put forward in favour of the reduced rate in these prodemographic products, are all right and are part of our argument at European Union level in bilateral discussions in Member States which, for example, have a Presidency in the European Union. For these prodemographic goods, this is not a significant budgetary impact. We estimate that this is about PLN 200 million per year. This is about a certain prodemographic coherence, a pro-family of Law and Justice and government policy. So that citizens are aware that at every stage this family with children is supported, whether we are talking about the 500+ programme, whether we are talking about a reduced rate on shoes and those clothes which, as the Member mentioned, must be exchanged frequently because of the growing child. So that in this respect the government is optimistic about the progress of work at European Union level and we should expect changes in this area soon. Turning to projects reducing the VAT rate during the tax year, during 2018, I would like to say that these projects generate significant budgetary damage. If we estimate the impact of reducing these rates annually to PLN 8.5 billion, then, respectively, there would be over 4 billion damage this year. These projects do not indicate the sources of funding for this damage, which means that they are in principle dangerous and pose a risk to public finances still this year. This means that from the perspective of the government, from the perspective of the Ministry of Finance, they are not accepted in the form in which they are presented. Of course, current budgetary results are the result of financial flows at this point in time, while, as a general rule, the 2018 budget provides for a deficit, and we cannot clearly claim that the current surplus allows to finance this loss which will lower the basic rate and lower the VAT rates to the budget. I would also like to remind you that the increase in the VAT rate in 2011 was not directly due to the global crisis of the world economy, the global financial crisis in the world, but, as you said here from the Platform, it was a rescue rate. It's a fantastic term for what happened back then. It was actually a rescue so that statistically it could prove itself [[♪ The bell ♪]] the financial situation that does not trigger the prudential thresholds. Today we have a different situation. Sealing the tax system, sealing the VAT system causes this additional percentage point to return to the pockets of Poles, to the pockets of Polish families, rather than to entities which abuse or extort VAT. And my last sentence. The argument that took place here, namely that simplifying the VAT rate matrix reduces the scale of fraud, is absolutely absurd, has nothing to do with reality. The definition of VAT rates does not affect fraud. If that were true, then the complexity of the VAT rate matrix for this hot dog would also result in VAT fraud in 2010 and 2011. That's not true. I think it's enough to look at these cases in the papers. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u320	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media on the draft resolution of the Polish Sejm on the commemoration of General Jan Henryk Dąbrowski on the 200th anniversary of his death. This project was submitted by Mr Bartłomiej Wróblewski from Poznań. No wonder Wielkopolska insists on commemorating General Dąbrowski. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating General John Henryk Dąbrowski on the 200th anniversary of his death. Gen. Jan Henryk Dąbrowski was an outstanding military commander and organizer of Polish military formations. During the second partition of Poland as one of the few commanders honorably resisted the Prussian troops in Gniezno, while during the Kościuszko Insurrection he not only marked himself during the defence of Warsaw, but also organized a successful diversionary expedition to Wielkopolska, giving the Prussian troops great losses. After the fall of the uprising, he went to France to emigrate. In 1797 he became the main organizer of fighting alongside Napoleon Polish Legions in Italy - the first Polish military formation after the fall of the Republic of Poland. He demonstrated excellent organizational skills there, implemented a modern command and promotion system, and provided soldiers with reading and writing lessons and patriotic education. The liquidation of the Legions did not end his sacrificial service. In 1806, he played a fundamental role in shaping Napoleon's policy towards Poles. The arrival of Jan Henryk Dąbrowski and Józef Wybicki to Poznań on 6 November 1806 met with great enthusiasm of society and gave rise to the armed movement which took over Wielkopolska and then the lands of central Poland. During that time, General Dąbrowski joined the organization of the Polish army and, within 2 months, put more than 20,000 under arms. soldiers, which was an outstanding achievement. The Wielkopolska Uprising was the first of the successful national uprisings and brought hope for the rebirth of Polish statehood. In 1812 he took part in an expedition to Moscow, and the following year, after the death of Fr. Józef Poniatowski became chief commander of the Army of the Duchy of Warsaw. After Napoleon's fall, he organized the army of the Polish Kingdom. He died on 6 June 1818 in his estate in Winnogóra in the Grand Duchy of Poznań, where he was buried. The urn with his heart is composed in the Crypt of Decent Great-Polans in the undergrounds of the church of St. Wojciech in Poznań. The achievements of General Jan Henryk Dąbrowski as the creators of the Legions led to the return of the Polish case to the international arena. The general's figure has become a symbol of the aspirations of independent Poles. It significantly contributed to the rebirth, although in incomplete form, of the Polish statehood in the form of the Duchy of Warsaw. 'The Song of Polish Legions in Italy' by Józef Wybicki, who after regaining independence became our national anthem, was called 'Mazurka Dąbrowski' from the very beginning. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to the figures and achievements of General Jan Henryk Dąbrowski on the 200th anniversary of his death. I would like to ask the High Sejm to accept the content of this resolution by acclamation. [[The gathered get up, long-lasting applause]] Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u384	Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Director! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Act on the National Institute of Freedom - Centre for Civil Society Development, print No. 2576. This draft law aims to increase the spending limits of the National Institute of Freedom. The National Freedom Institute is implementing a state policy for the development of civil society based on the principles of subsidiarity, partnership, efficiency, competitiveness and transparency. The National Freedom Institute manages civil society development programmes. The task of the National Institute of Freedom is to implement actions to promote the development of the civil community and civil society in the Republic of Poland, in particular by enhancing the institutional efficiency of NGOs and other organised forms of civil society, their independence and professionalism, while preserving their civil character. It is also the task of the National Institute to strengthen the capacity of NGOs and other organised forms of civil society. In addition, the main tasks of the National Institute are to support the involvement of citizens, NGOs and other organised forms of civil society in public life, as well as processes for shaping public policies and decision-making; to support citizens' control over the functioning of public institutions and public trust institutions and to increase their transparency and compliance with the principles of good governance; to promote grassroots civic activity; to strengthen and release the reserves of social ethos of citizens; to promote and promote volunteering; to maintain and disseminate culture and national and local traditions; to cultivate Polishness and to develop national, civic and cultural awareness; and to promote solidarity and social sensitivity; to promote respect and protection of human rights and civil rights; to educate citizens and to develop civic attitudes; to promote the protection of the environment and animal rights. In May 2018, after prior agreement with the members of the Council of Ministers and holding a public consultation with non-governmental organisations and an opinion with other public administrations... These are government programmes, civil society development programmes within the meaning of Article. Article 23 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1th Act: The Civil Organisation Development Programme where support will be available for: mission implementation and institutional development of civic organisations, building the financial stability of civil organisations, funding the contribution of own organisations applying for funding from international programmes, the operation of civil and local media; The Boy Scouts Development Programme; the Solidarity Corps development programme. The total budget of the National Institute of Freedom for grants from the state budget and the system of gambling aid under the Civil Society Development Fund will amount to about PLN 100.140 million per year. The implementation of these programmes requires an appropriate and efficient managing authority. The impact assessment on the draft law provides that the funds from the subjective grant of PLN 3.6 million per year are insufficient and allow the creation of around PLN 3.6 million. 22 jobs, and 60 jobs are needed to carry out all tasks of the National Freedom Institute. In order to guarantee the proper and professional performance of the tasks, it is necessary to increase and provide new sources of funding for the National Institute of Freedom. In order to allow for the adoption of an increased grant under consideration by the National Freedom Institute, the draft law amends the article. 46. 1 law by increasing expenditure limits of the National Institute of Freedom. In addition, the revised provision takes into account the costs of renovating the park for the permanent seat of the National Institute of Liberty. The provision also takes into account the annual increase from 2019 in the expenditure limit under the special-purpose subsidy for the implementation of the increased number of tasks of the national institute. High Izbo! Following a thorough analysis of the proposed law, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u400	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! High Seym! I have the honour to present a draft law amending the Local referendum law and the National referendum law adopted by the Senate in its resolution and contained in print No 566. This, of course, was also sent to the Sejm. The purpose of this submission is to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Court. This is the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 20 April 2017, sygn. Act K 10/15, declaring that the provisions of the Act of 14 March 2003 on a national referendum and the Act of 15 September 2000 on a local referendum are incompatible with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. It would be an art. Article 44 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 The second sentence of the National referendum Act to the extent that it excludes the admissibility of the reopening of proceedings and Article 3 Article 35 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3rd sentence of the Law of 15 September 2000 on a local referendum in so far as it excludes the admissibility of the reopening of proceedings. As the Constitutional Tribunal states, these are not compatible with the Article. 45 constitutions, that is, the article that says that everyone has the right to an open and fair examination of their case and is incompatible with the article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Two constitutions, which this article says, that the bill cannot close anybody's judicial path. I would like to point out that in the provisions which have now been contested, Article. 35 states that the decision of the district court within 24 hours of its delivery serves to appeal to the court of appeal. The Court of Appeal of three Judges shall recognise the complaint in non-trial proceedings in the procedure and within the time limit referred to in paragraph. 2, which means within 24 hours. The decision of the Court of Appeal shall not be subject to appeal and shall be immediately enforced. By contrast, Article 4 (1) (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 should be amended accordingly. 44 also points out such time limits as to the fact that the decision of the district court shall be subject to a complaint within 24 hours before the appeal court, which shall consider the complaint within 24 hours, and also in the same way as in the case of Article. 44, the decision of the Court of Appeal shall not be subject to a legal measure and shall be immediately enforced. The Senate has proposed such a solution, so that in the article. Article 35 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 In the third sentence of the third sentence, the wording of the third sentence was drafted precisely in such a way that the decision of the Court of Appeal is not subject to a cassation appeal and is immediately enforceable. At this point we delete the words accordingly: there is no legal measure, in one law, in the other we delete: there is no appeal, but we write in this place that there is no cassation action, i.e. there is no cassation action in the local referendum law and there is no cassation action in the national referendum law. That means that all other remedies and remedies are available, which means that the right to resume... This gives us the opportunity to resume the proceedings. The Constitutional Tribunal has taken the view that the deadlines contained in the local referendum and national referendum laws are very short terms. This is due to the atmosphere that it is then, to a referendum, i.e. quick deadlines and when the issue of false entries in leaflets or posters is raised, it requires quick decisions. The Constitutional Tribunal has taken the view that maintaining the speed of these time limits must not render the person concerned deprived of the right to review, the possibility of appeal, the exercise of a legal remedy or the possibility to challenge that judgment. So that I ask the Sejm to accept this legislative proposal, which we are presenting as the Senate. It is the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Court, of that judgment which I referred to here. I'm asking you to analyze the solution we're proposing that this sentence be executed. Our proposal is as I just presented it. Thank you, Marshal. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u402	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Club Law and Justice a position regarding the draft law amending the Local referendum law and the National referendum law, print No 2434. The aim of the project is to introduce the possibility of reopening proceedings after the decision of the referenda court in matters relating to the dissemination of false information in statements, leaflets, that is what you just spoke about during the referenda campaign. The draft adapts the system of law to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 20 April 2017 declaring that the provisions of the National referendum Act of 14 March 2003 and the Local referendum of 15 September 2000 are incompatible with the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. Currently, a case may be brought before the Court of Appeal, i.e. the Court of Appeal, before the Second Court, but it is not possible to request the reopening of proceedings. The proposed law will introduce this possibility so that such activities can take place in law. The reopening institution is intended to enable, in particularly justified cases, the reopening of a case which has already been brought to an end by a final and final judgment. The action for reopening proceedings is therefore an exception to the principle of the stability of final decisions, which is essential from a formal point of view as regards the rule of law - the principles of protection of trust in the State and the law and the right to settle a judicial case within a reasonable period of time. As for the other details, I will no longer bring them closer, because the senator has presented these points and paragraphs. On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I ask the High House to proceed over this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-06-06-sejm-63-2.u408	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Senate draft law amending the Local referendum law and the National referendum law. The draft law prepared by the group of senators aims to align the law with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 20 April 2017, which states that the provisions of the National referendum Act and the Local referendum Act are incompatible with the Constitution. The Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment. Act K 10/15 found the provisions of Article incompatible with the Constitution. Article 44 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3nd sentence of the National referendum Act and Article. Article 35 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3rd sentence of the Local referendum Act, according to which no appeal in the form of an application for the reopening of proceedings is granted from the provisions of the appeal court in matters relating to the dissemination of false information in referenda material. At present, both laws assume that where materials relating to national or local referendums, e.g. Posters, leaflets, passwords and statements contain false information, each interested party has the opportunity to apply for a decision to the district court, among others. prohibit the dissemination of such information, order the rectification of such information and apologies to the person whose personal property has been violated. The district court shall recognise the application within 24 hours in non-trial proceedings. The proceedings of the district court within 24 hours of its date of issue shall be served by appeal to the court of appeal. The Court of Appeal of 3 Judges shall recognise a complaint in non-trial proceedings, including within 24 hours. The proceedings of the Court of Appeal shall be immediately enforced and shall not be subject to an appeal, which means that, unlike the provisions of the Civil Procedure Code, the proceedings may not be renewed if such factual circumstances or means of evidence are detected which could affect the outcome of the case and which the party could not have benefited from earlier. The Constitutional Tribunal pointed out that in the so-called. referenda proceedings have very short time limits for the examination of the case at first and second instance to ensure that a decision is given in such a time that voters can, on the one hand, read the findings of the court before the day of the vote and, on the other hand, that a fair pre-election debate, free from pathology related to the dissemination of false messages, takes place for a sufficiently long period of time to form the will of voters. At the same time, the Constitutional Tribunal noted that the simplification and shortening of proceedings cannot lead to a finding that is not true, which does not correspond to the requirements of the rule of law. He pointed out that it should be possible to touch the final judgment of the court, which is seriously flawed. This applies in particular to a situation in which new evidence will emerge after the judgment has been given, which shows that the final judgment does not correspond to reality. The Constitutional Tribunal also noted that, in the case of electoral proceedings, the risk of misconceptions is significantly increased due to their speed. It is worth noting that, in 1470 cases relating to the dissemination of false information in electoral material conducted in 2014 and 2017 electoral mode, i.e. concerning the 2014 local elections and the European Parliament elections and the 2015 presidential and parliamentary elections, the resumption of proceedings was requested only in four cases. The proposed regulation will allow individuals whose personal property has been affected by the dissemination of false information in referendum campaigns, to request that proceedings be reopened in the event of a court order being issued in referenda mode in the above mentioned above. cases, e.g. in the event of the discovery of such facts or evidence which could affect the outcome of the case and which the party could not have benefited from earlier. The draft law is extremely important and is moving towards the execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. It is important because it is about fulfilling justice related to the essence of the case, investigating the truth. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Polish Free and Solidarni Circle will support the Senate's draft law on amending the Local referendum law and the National referendum law. Thank you very much.
